Nobody’s really told me that my speech is hard to follow. It’s more that everything feels really slow, especially my hands at work—like sectioning hair or mixing color takes way longer than it should, and people have noticed I’m slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energized or high and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I stayed up late, but not where I felt unusually high or energized and didn’t need sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days I wake up already feeling really low. It’s like this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, and I keep thinking there’s no point in trying to build the life I thought I was going to have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Like when I’m sweeping up or rinsing bowls at the salon, I’ll hear a woman’s voice near the shampoo stations saying I’m ruining everything. But then I look, and the salon’s empty, or nobody’s anywhere close by.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Before the depression really got bad, there were a few weeks where I was still getting up and going to work like normal, or mostly normal. But I was already hearing that voice, and I was convinced someone at the salon was messing with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that your thoughts seem jumbled?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Nobody’s really told me that my speech is hard to follow. I mean, sometimes I lose my train of thought, but I haven’t noticed people saying I sound jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in seeing other people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I used to like talking with clients and helping the newer stylists at work. I’d go hiking with friends too. Lately I mostly just keep my head down and avoid people. I don’t really have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before you started feeling depressed, were you already pulling away from people or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think it started around then, yeah. I used to be more social at work—chatting with clients, helping the newer stylists—and I’d go hiking with friends. But I started just keeping my head down and avoiding people. It was like I didn’t have that spark anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before you started feeling depressed, did you already feel emotionally flat or like you had very little motivation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Even before I felt really depressed, I was already kind of pulling back. It was like I didn’t have that spark anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before you started feeling depressed, did you already feel emotionally flat or like you had very little motivation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I just didn’t have that spark or motivation like I used to.\n\nDoctor: For at least 2 weeks, have you heard that voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. Before the depression really took over, there were a few weeks where I was still getting up and working, functioning more normally, but I was already hearing the voice. I also felt really sure someone at the salon was messing with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you held a strange posture or stayed very still for a long time, even when there was no clear reason to?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I might sit around when I’m feeling low, but not like being stuck in a posture or staying frozen for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were interfering with your schedule, but you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. Like I said, before the depression really hit, there were a few weeks where I was still getting up and going to work, doing things more normally. But I was already hearing the voice, and I felt pretty sure someone at the salon was messing with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: At the same time you were hearing the voice, were you completely free of depressed mood?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say completely free, but before the depression really took over there was a stretch of several weeks where I was still getting up and functioning pretty normally. During that time I was already hearing the voice and feeling sure someone at the salon was interfering with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: For at least 1 month, have you been hearing that voice or believing someone at the salon was interfering with your schedule?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been way longer than a month. The voice and that feeling that someone is targeting my bookings at the salon have been going on for about a year and four months now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
